# One programmable chip for protocols: a protocol-emulator ASIC

This is the working repository for an entry to Jane Street's
[protocol-emulator ASIC competition](https://blog.janestreet.com/protocol-emulator-asic-competition/):
a small chip, fabricated on IHP's 130 nm process through a Tiny Tapeout shuttle, that speaks
protocols such as UART, SPI and I2C on behalf of a host. Entries are due on 18 January 2027, for
the March 2027 shuttle, in at most 6x4 tiles.

The design takes the competition's hint literally: "The goal isn't to put a UART block, an SPI
block, and an I2C block on one die and call it done." There are no protocol blocks. The chip is a handful of generic
programmable blocks, and every protocol and every demo is a programme or a configuration of them.
Timing is exact by construction, so a compiler can check before anything runs that a programme
meets its deadlines.

Everything here is design, simulation and measurement. Nothing has been fabricated, and the FPGA
bring-up is prepared but not yet run on a board.

## The intended chip

This is the target design; the blocks exist as separate prototypes and are not yet integrated
into one top level.

```
            host (the RP2040/RP2350 on Tiny Tapeout's demo board)
                                 | programme and configuration loading, data streams
   +-----------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
   | sequencer: 4 hardware threads, deadline waits ------------ programme memory    |
   |    | push/pull bus                  | pin writes and reads           | mailbox |
   |    v                                v                                          |
   | PE array: 16 processing elements, pin stage: four-phase output and input,      |
   | split into segments, with         open drain, complementary pairs, streamer,  |
   | loop-backs and feeds   <------->  sampler, phase accumulator (pin NCO)        |
   |    ^                               ^  bit-path assists: edge-tracking sampler, |
   |    |                               |  bit-stuff tracker, line coder, CRC,      |
   | gain-cell memory banks             |  sync-word matcher                        |
   +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
          one 60 MHz clock, four phases derived on chip
```

- **Deadline sequencer.** Four hardware threads issue round-robin, one instruction per clock in
  total, so every instruction takes exactly one slot. Its central instruction is "wait for this
  pin level, but no longer than this deadline". Threads pass data through small mailboxes. The executable specification is an OCaml
  interpreter (`prototypes/sequencer-v2/isa2.ml`); the hardware is written in
  [Hardcaml](https://github.com/janestreet/hardcaml).
- **Four-phase pin stage.** Four clock phases let a pin change, or be sampled, on a quarter-clock
  grid: four edges per clock out, 4x oversampling in (`prototypes/multiphase`).
- **Pin stage helpers.** A streamer and a sampler move words between the host and the pins at a
  fixed rate without the sequencer; a phase accumulator (NCO) makes carriers and PWM; small assists
  handle bit recovery, bit stuffing, line coding, CRCs and sync-word matching.
- **Processing-element array.** Sixteen identical elements in a line (a systolic array), which can
  be split into independent segments, for the work a sequencer is too slow for: CRCs, correlation,
  phase accumulators, pixel generation, audio noise shaping (`prototypes/unified-pe`). Its RTL is
  checked in lockstep against a model (`prototypes/unified-pe/verify`).
- **Gain-cell memory.** Dynamic memory cells denser than the process's SRAM, which forget within
  microseconds to milliseconds; the compiler schedules every read before its value expires
  (`prototypes/gain-cell`, `notes/gain-cell-compiler.md`). So far these are SPICE simulations on
  the process models and drawn layouts with DRC and LVS runs, not part of any hardened design.

The design rationale, including how the blocks were chosen by counting the primitives the earlier
special-purpose prototypes needed, is in `notes/architecture-v0.md`. Some earlier prototypes ran
at other clock rates (53.2 MHz for PAL video, for instance); the note explains the choice of
60 MHz. The running list of open
work is `notes/backlog.md`.

## What runs on it (in simulation)

| Area | Protocols and demos | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Competition list | UART, SPI, I2C | `prototypes/deadline-sequencer`, `prototypes/sequencer-v2` |
| Further protocols | JTAG, SWD, PS/2, CAN, S/PDIF | `prototypes/proto-jtag-swd`, `prototypes/sequencer-ps2-can`, `prototypes/spdif` |
| Stretch goals | low-speed USB as firmware; 10BASE-T Ethernet answering ARP and ping | `prototypes/usb-ls`, `prototypes/eth10-node` |
| Several at once | several protocols on one sequencer, with bridges between them (Ethernet to TV, a CAN bus analyser) | `prototypes/multi-proto` |
| Video | PAL and NTSC composite colour, a retro console, a platformer game, a wave-interference game | `prototypes/retro-console`, `prototypes/platformer`, `prototypes/wave-engine`, `prototypes/video-nco` |
| Audio and radio | one-bit audio output with noise shaping, IMA ADPCM and SBC decoding, FM transmit | `prototypes/onebit-dac`, `prototypes/multiphase` |
| Gadgets | a GPS finder that tells you by sound whether you are getting closer, the chip as a logic analyser and oscilloscope | `prototypes/gps-hotcold`, `prototypes/scope` |
| FPGA test bench | ULX3S top level, host runner and checker; DVI output to a monitor | `prototypes/fpga-ulx3s`, `prototypes/hdmi-ulx3s` |

Each directory's README states what was checked, how, and what was not.

## How it is checked

The aim is that no claim rests on one implementation agreeing with itself, and that every check is
shown to be able to fail.

- **Lockstep.** The RTL runs clock by clock against the OCaml specification and every output is
  compared. Each test suite comes with deliberately planted bugs that it must catch.
- **Independent oracles.** Protocol traffic is judged by code written separately from the design:
  host models, [sigrok](https://sigrok.org/)'s protocol decoders run as an external program
  (`tools/sigrok-judge`), and unmodified third-party HDL such as alexforencich's `verilog-uart`
  (`tools/peers`). Every judge also gets a deliberately faulty trace, to show it can fail.
- **A static programme verifier.** Abstract interpretation proves that a programme makes its pin
  events within the declared timing windows for every input, or rejects it with the path to the
  violation (`prototypes/verifier`). It proves all 530 programmes the compiler emits across the
  swept parameter ranges, and its trusted kernel is tested with planted kernel bugs.
- **Formal proofs.** Bounded model checking of the specification with z3; SymbiYosys and Yosys
  proofs on the RTL, including that the chip behaves identically after reset whatever its
  registers held at power-up; an equivalence check of our UART against the transmitter in Jane
  Street's [`hardcaml_hobby_boards`](https://github.com/janestreet/hardcaml_hobby_boards). Every property is reported as proved, reachable or vacuous
  (`prototypes/formal`).
- **Layout back to behaviour.** A netlist extracted from the final GDS alone, independently of the
  place-and-route tools, is compared with the place-and-route record per cell and per net, and run
  cycle by cycle against the RTL (`prototypes/postlayout-roundtrip`).
- **Coverage-guided fuzzing** of Hardcaml designs (`prototypes/hwfuzz`), stall injection, and
  checks that every pin and memory bank has one declared owner (`prototypes/verif-oracles`).

## Tiny Tapeout

`tt/` is the submission harness, laid out as in Tiny Tapeout's IHP template. Its Verilog is
generated from Hardcaml at build time and never committed. `tt/scripts/harden.sh` runs Tiny
Tapeout's own hardening steps in a pinned copy of their LibreLane environment
(`tools/librelane-tt`). The first run, on a 6x4-tile placeholder top level around the sequencer,
finished with no DRC, LVS or antenna errors, setup slack +10.39 ns and hold slack +0.12 ns at a
20 ns clock (`tools/librelane-tt/results/tt-harden/`). It has not been through Tiny Tapeout's
precheck yet, and the real top level, with all the blocks above, is not integrated yet.

## Repository map

| Path | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `prototypes/` | one directory per block, protocol, demo or study, each with a README and a `results/` directory |
| `tools/` | the sigrok judge, third-party peers, the pinned LibreLane environment, latency checks |
| `tt/` | the Tiny Tapeout harness |
| `notes/` | architecture, backlog, prior-art surveys, and what we learnt from other entrants and from the solvers of Jane Street's earlier [ASIC puzzle](https://blog.janestreet.com/asic-puzzle-results/) |
| `measurements/` | early area studies: an RP2040 PIO clone and a FABulous embedded-FPGA tile on the same process |
| `brainstorm-*.md`, `synthesis.md`, `second-layer/` | the opening brainstorm (see "How this was made") |
| `datapoints.md`, `PLAN.md`, `techniques.md` | early measured facts, the first plan, and ways a large host can do the work a tiny chip cannot |

## Reproducing

Most prototypes are OCaml: install Hardcaml v0.17 in an opam switch with OCaml 5.3, then
`dune build` in the prototype's directory; its README lists the commands, and each `results/`
file names the command and commit that produced it. Synthesis and place and route use Yosys and
LibreLane in containers; the Python tools run through [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/). The IHP
process design kit and third-party sources are not vendored unless stated; the notes name the
commits used.

## How this was made

The work was done with AI assistants, which wrote much of the code and the first drafts of the
notes: mainly Claude, with Codex, Kimi and DeepSeek for brainstorming and adversarial reviews. The brainstorm files at the top level are three
independent idea lists written by Claude, Codex and Kimi from the same prompt, compared in
`synthesis.md`, plus `brainstorm-wild.md`, which deliberately ignores where they agree. The
verification above is set up so that no result depends on trusting any one model's output.

## Credits

Ideas and code from other people's public work are credited where they are used, by repository,
and listed with their licences in `notes/learned-from-others.md`,
`notes/learned-from-puzzle-solvers.md` and `NOTICE`. Prior-art surveys are in `notes/prior-art-*.md`.

## Licence

Apache-2.0 (see `LICENSE`); the repository was relicensed from MIT on 2026-09-25. Third-party files keep their
own licences, listed in `NOTICE`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	(* Executable specification of the whole chip core, one clock per [step].
     2	
     3	   It is composed from the blocks' own models, which are not written anew here:
     4	   - the sequencer: ISA v2's interpreter (Isa2, ../sequencer-v2/isa2.ml);
     5	   - the PE array: Upe.Model (../unified-pe/verify/model.ml);
     6	   - the streamer and the sampler: Pstream.Model and Psample.Model;
     7	   - the edge-tracking sampler and the CRC unit: the models in Eth10.Edge_sampler and
     8	     Eth10.Crc_unit; the matcher: the closed form of Eth10.Model.
     9	   New here, because nothing specified them before: the glue (host link, its FIFOs, the register
    10	   file, the pin map and pad sources, the flag selects, the port holds between the threads and
    11	   the array, the memories) and the pin NCO, which has RTL (../multiphase/nco.ml) but no model.
    12	   The NCO's behaviour is written below from nco.ml's header comment.
    13	
    14	   The boundary is the four-phase stage's (README, "Phases"): inputs are, per input pad, the
    15	   four quarter samples the core sees in this clock (bit p = quarter p); outputs are, per output
    16	   pad, the nibble of levels for the next clock's four quarters, and the uio output enables.
    17	   Outputs depend only on the state at the start of the clock.
    18	
    19	   Within a clock everything reads the state as it was at the start of the clock, and every
    20	   update lands at its end, as registers do. Where models must be stepped in some order, the
    21	   order below is the one in which their inputs become available, and no model reads another's
    22	   updated state. *)
    23	
    24	open Regs
    25	module UM = Upe.Model
    26	module US = Upe.Spec
    27	module SM = Pstream.Model
    28	module PM = Psample.Model
    29	module ES = Eth10.Edge_sampler
    30	module CRC = Eth10.Crc_unit
    31	module MM = Eth10.Model
    32	
    33	type config = { layout : US.layout; prog_words : int }
    34	
    35	let default_config = { layout = US.layout_of_sizes [| 1; 1; 1; 1 |]; prog_words = 1024 }
    36	let bank_bytes = Isa2.bank_len
    37	let hostin_depth = 16 and hostout_depth = 8
    38	
    39	(* a host-link byte that was decoded in the previous clock and acts in this one *)
    40	type action =
    41	  | A_reg of int * int            (* address, byte *)
    42	  | A_prog of int * int
    43	  | A_bank of int * int
    44	  | A_hostin of int
    45	  | A_pecfg of int * int          (* segment, byte *)
    46	  | A_peinit of int * int
    47	  | A_peseg of int * int * int    (* segment, sel, byte *)
    48	  | A_stream of int * int         (* address mod 4, byte *)
    49	  | A_match of int                (* bit *)
    50	
    51	type parser = Idle | Addr_hi | Addr_lo | Count | Wdata | Rdata
    52	
    53	type t = {
    54	  cfg : config;
    55	  regs : int array;
    56	  mutable err_mem : bool; mutable hin_ovf : bool; mutable hout_ovf : bool; mutable act_drop : bool;
    57	  prog : int array;
    58	  bank : int array;
    59	  mutable seq : Isa2.state;
    60	  hostin : int Queue.t;
    61	  hostout : (int * int) Queue.t;
    62	  mutable out_pend : (int * int) option;     (* the core's OUT, registered; enters the FIFO now *)
    63	  mutable push_pend : (int * int) option;    (* the core's SEND to a port, registered *)
    64	  send_hi : bool array;                      (* per port: the next SEND byte is the high byte *)
    65	  hold_st : int array;                       (* per port: 0 empty, 1 low byte next, 2 high byte next *)
    66	  hold_w : int array;
    67	  pe : UM.t;
    68	  mutable str : SM.t;
    69	  mutable smp : PM.t;
    70	  mutable es : ES.st;
    71	  mutable es_out : ES.out;
    72	  mutable es_last : int;                     (* the last recovered bit, held between bits *)
    73	  mutable crc : int;
    74	  chain : int array;                         (* the matcher's 37 configuration bits, in chain order *)
    75	  mutable mhist : int array;
    76	  mutable mtime : int;
    77	  mutable nco_acc : int;
    78	  mutable nco_out : int;
    79	  (* host link *)
    80	  mutable prev_s : int; mutable prev_r : int;
    81	  mutable nphase : int; mutable hi_nib : int;
    82	  mutable pst : parser; mutable op : int; mutable tgt : int; mutable addr : int;
    83	  mutable remaining : int;
    84	  mutable rbuf : int; mutable rphase : int;
    85	  mutable pend_action : action option;
    86	  mutable pend_fetch : (int * int) option;   (* target, address *)
    87	  mutable prog_lo : int; mutable str_lo : int; mutable str_hi : int;
    88	  mutable hout_lat : bool; mutable smp_lat : bool;
    89	  mutable last_fetch : (int * int * int) option;   (* for diagnostics *)
    90	}
    91	
    92	let rep4 b = if b <> 0 then 15 else 0
    93	let bit v i = (v lsr i) land 1
    94	let u32 v = v land 0xFFFF_FFFF
    95	let reg32 t a = t.regs.(a) lor (t.regs.(a + 1) lsl 8) lor (t.regs.(a + 2) lsl 16) lor (t.regs.(a + 3) lsl 24)
    96	
    97	(* ---- block configurations from the registers ---- *)
    98	
    99	let streamer_cfg t : SM.cfg =
   100	  let r = t.regs in
   101	  { period = r.(r_str_per) lor ((r.(r_str_per + 1) land 15) lsl 8); width = (r.(r_str_per + 1) lsr 4) land 7;
   102	    od_mask = r.(r_str_pins) land 15; idle_out = (r.(r_str_pins) lsr 4) land 15; idle_oe = r.(r_str_idle_oe) land 15 }
   103	
   104	let sampler_cfg t : PM.cfg =
   105	  let r = t.regs in
   106	  { clocked = bit r.(r_smp_per + 1) 7 = 1; period = r.(r_smp_per) lor ((r.(r_smp_per + 1) land 15) lsl 8);
   107	    width = (r.(r_smp_per + 1) lsr 4) land 7; trig_pin = (r.(r_smp_off + 1) lsr 4) land 3;
   108	    trig_val = bit r.(r_smp_off + 1) 6; offset = r.(r_smp_off) lor ((r.(r_smp_off + 1) land 15) lsl 8);
   109	    frame_len = r.(r_smp_frame) }
   110	
   111	let es_cfg t : ES.cfg =
   112	  let r = t.regs in
   113	  { mode = (match r.(r_es_mode) land 3 with 0 -> ES.Manchester | 2 -> ES.Nrz | _ -> ES.Biphase_mark);
   114	    invert = bit r.(r_es_mode) 2 = 1;
   115	    holdoff = r.(r_es_holdoff) lor ((r.(r_es_holdoff + 1) land 3) lsl 8);
   116	    timeout = r.(r_es_timeout) lor ((r.(r_es_timeout + 1) land 3) lsl 8);
   117	    offset = r.(r_es_offset); period = r.(r_es_period) }
   118	
   119	(* The CRC unit's model takes a width; the RTL takes a mask. The host must write a mask of the
   120	   form 2^w - 1 (README); the width is then the number of ones. *)
   121	let crc_cfg t : CRC.cfg =
   122	  let mask = reg32 t r_crc_mask in
   123	  let rec ones v = if v = 0 then 0 else (v land 1) + ones (v lsr 1) in
   124	  { width = max 1 (ones mask); poly = reg32 t r_crc_poly; init = reg32 t r_crc_init;
   125	    reflected = bit t.regs.(r_crc_ctrl) 0 = 1; xorout = reg32 t r_crc_xorout; residue = reg32 t r_crc_residue }
   126	
   127	let matcher_cfg t : MM.cfg =
   128	  { t = Array.init MM.n (fun i -> t.chain.(2 * i + 1)); m = Array.init MM.n (fun i -> t.chain.(2 * i));
   129	    thr = Array.fold_left ( lor ) 0 (Array.init 5 (fun k -> t.chain.(32 + k) lsl k)) }
   130	
   131	let matcher_now t =
   132	  let c = matcher_cfg t and x k = t.mhist.(k) in
   133	  (MM.y_at c x t.mtime, MM.hit_at c x t.mtime)
   134	
   135	let boot t = Array.init 4 (fun th -> ((t.regs.(r_boot_page) lsr (2 * th)) land 3, t.regs.(r_boot_pc th)))
   136	
   137	(* coverage of chip-level events, for the lockstep's report (not part of the behaviour) *)
   138	let cov : (string, int) Hashtbl.t = Hashtbl.create 64
   139	let hit name = Hashtbl.replace cov name (1 + Option.value ~default:0 (Hashtbl.find_opt cov name))
   140	
   141	let es_zero : ES.out = { bit = 0; valid = false; burst_end = false; overrun = false }
   142	
   143	(* The edge sampler's bit output is defined only while its valid is high; the glue holds the
   144	   last recovered bit, so pads, flags and the sampler see a level. *)
   145	let es_level t = if t.es_out.valid then t.es_out.bit else t.es_last
   146	
   147	let create ?(cfg = default_config) () =
   148	  let regs = Array.init reg_space reset_value in
   149	  let t =
   150	    { cfg; regs; err_mem = false; hin_ovf = false; hout_ovf = false; act_drop = false;
   151	      prog = Array.make cfg.prog_words 0; bank = Array.make bank_bytes 0;
   152	      seq = Isa2.Spec.reset ~boot:(Array.make 4 (0, 0)) ~bank:(Array.make bank_bytes 0);
   153	      hostin = Queue.create (); hostout = Queue.create (); out_pend = None; push_pend = None;
   154	      send_hi = Array.make 4 false; hold_st = Array.make 4 0; hold_w = Array.make 4 0;
   155	      pe = UM.create ~layout:cfg.layout ();
   156	      str = SM.create { period = 1; width = 1; od_mask = 0; idle_out = 0; idle_oe = 0 };
   157	      smp = PM.create { clocked = false; period = 1; width = 1; trig_pin = 0; trig_val = 0; offset = 0; frame_len = 0 };
   158	      es = ES.init (); es_out = es_zero; es_last = 0; crc = 0; chain = Array.make 37 0; mhist = Array.make 1024 0;
   159	      mtime = 0; nco_acc = 0; nco_out = 0; prev_s = 0; prev_r = 0; nphase = 0; hi_nib = 0; pst = Idle;
   160	      op = 0; tgt = 0; addr = 0; remaining = 0; rbuf = 0; rphase = 0; pend_action = None;
   161	      pend_fetch = None; prog_lo = 0; str_lo = 0; str_hi = 0; hout_lat = false; smp_lat = false; last_fetch = None }
   162	  in
   163	  t.seq <- Isa2.Spec.reset ~boot:(boot t) ~bank:t.bank;
   164	  t.str <- SM.create (streamer_cfg t);
   165	  t.smp <- PM.create (sampler_cfg t);
   166	  t
   167	
   168	(* ---- outputs: from the state at the start of the clock ---- *)
   169	
   170	type outputs = { pad_nib : int array; uio_oe : int }
   171	
   172	let taps t = (UM.state t.pe).taps
   173	
   174	let source t (taps : US.tap array) src =
   175	  if src < 8 then ((t.seq.pin_sub lsr (4 * src)) land 15, bit t.seq.pin_oe src)
   176	  else if src < 12 then (rep4 (bit t.str.out (src - 8)), bit t.str.oe (src - 8))
   177	  else if src = src_nco_quarter then (t.nco_out land 15, 1)
   178	  else if src = src_nco_half then ((t.nco_out lsr 4) land 15, 1)
   179	  else if src = src_nco_grid then ((t.nco_out lsr 8) land 15, 1)
   180	  else if src >= 16 && src < 20 then (rep4 (Bool.to_int taps.(src - 16).tf), 1)
   181	  else if src >= 20 && src < 24 then (rep4 (taps.(src - 20).td land 1), 1)
   182	  else if src = src_es_bit then (rep4 (es_level t), 1)
   183	  else (0, 1)
   184	
   185	let outputs t =
   186	  let taps = taps t in
   187	  let pad_nib = Array.make n_pads 0 and uio_oe = ref 0 in
   188	  List.iter
   189	    (fun p ->
   190	      let sel = t.regs.(r_padsel p) in
   191	      let nib, oe = source t taps (sel land 31) in
   192	      let nib = if bit sel 5 = 1 then nib lxor 15 else nib in
   193	      if p < 8 then pad_nib.(p) <- (if oe = 1 then nib else rep4 (bit sel 6))
   194	      else begin
   195	        pad_nib.(p) <- nib;
   196	        if oe = 1 then uio_oe := !uio_oe lor (1 lsl (p - 8))
   197	      end)
   198	    general_out_pads;
   199	  (* host data: the current read nibble, driven while the host's read request is seen *)
   200	  let rnib = if t.rphase = 0 then t.rbuf lsr 4 else t.rbuf land 15 in
   201	  for k = 0 to 3 do pad_nib.(pad_hdata + k) <- rep4 (bit rnib k) done;
   202	  if t.prev_r = 1 then uio_oe := !uio_oe lor 0xF;
   203	  { pad_nib; uio_oe = !uio_oe }
   204	
   205	let flags t (taps : US.tap array) =
   206	  let src s =
   207	    if s < 4 then taps.(s).tf
   208	    else if s < 8 then taps.(s - 4).tv
   209	    else if s = fl_es_valid then t.es_out.valid
   210	    else if s = fl_es_bit then es_level t = 1
   211	    else if s = fl_es_in_burst then t.es.in_burst
   212	    else if s = fl_es_burst_end then t.es_out.burst_end
   213	    else if s = fl_crc_check then t.crc = (crc_cfg t).residue
   214	    else if s = fl_match_hit then snd (matcher_now t)
   215	    else if s = fl_stream_full then SM.full t.str
   216	    else if s = fl_sample_valid then not (Queue.is_empty t.smp.fifo)
   217	    else if s = fl_nco then bit t.nco_out 0 = 1
   218	    else false
   219	  in
   220	  let v = ref 0 in
   221	  for f = 0 to 15 do if src (t.regs.(r_flagsel f) land 31) then v := !v lor (1 lsl f) done;
   222	  !v
   223	
   224	(* ---- host-link reads ---- *)
   225	
   226	let run t = bit t.regs.(r_ctrl) 0 = 1
   227	let held t = bit t.regs.(r_ctrl) 1 = 1
   228	
   229	let status t =
   230	  Bool.to_int t.err_mem lor (Bool.to_int t.hin_ovf lsl 1) lor (Bool.to_int t.hout_ovf lsl 2)
   231	  lor (Bool.to_int (Queue.length t.hostin >= hostin_depth) lsl 3)
   232	  lor (Bool.to_int (not (Queue.is_empty t.hostout)) lsl 4)
   233	  lor (Bool.to_int (not (Queue.is_empty t.smp.fifo)) lsl 5)
   234	  lor (Bool.to_int (SM.full t.str) lsl 6) lor (Bool.to_int t.act_drop lsl 7)
   235	
   236	let read_reg t a =
   237	  if a >= reg_space then 0
   238	  else if rw_bits a > 0 then t.regs.(a)
   239	  else if a = r_status then status t
   240	  else if a = r_hostin_count then Queue.length t.hostin
   241	  else if a = r_hostout_count then Queue.length t.hostout
   242	  else if a >= r_crc_raw && a < r_crc_raw + 4 then (t.crc lsr (8 * (a - r_crc_raw))) land 0xFF
   243	  else if a = r_match then (let y, hit = matcher_now t in y lor (Bool.to_int hit lsl 7))
   244	  else if a = r_smp_overflows then t.smp.overflows land 0xFF
   245	  else 0
   246	
   247	(* the byte a fetch returns, and whether it pops the sampler *)
   248	let fetch t (tgt, a) =
   249	  let a = a land 0xFFFF in
   250	  if tgt = t_reg then (read_reg t a, false)
   251	  else if tgt = t_prog then
   252	    if run t then (t.err_mem <- true; (0, false))
   253	    else
   254	      let w = t.prog.((a lsr 1) land (t.cfg.prog_words - 1)) in
   255	      ((if a land 1 = 0 then w else w lsr 8) land 0xFF, false)
   256	  else if tgt = t_bank then
   257	    if run t then (t.err_mem <- true; (0, false)) else (t.bank.(a land (bank_bytes - 1)), false)
   258	  else if tgt = t_hostout then
   259	    if a land 1 = 0 then begin
   260	      t.hout_lat <- not (Queue.is_empty t.hostout);
   261	      ((match Queue.peek_opt t.hostout with Some (tag, _) -> 0x80 lor tag | None -> 0), false)
   262	    end
   263	    else if t.hout_lat then (t.hout_lat <- false; (snd (Queue.pop t.hostout), false))
   264	    else begin
   265	      if not (Queue.is_empty t.hostout) then hit "HOSTOUT entry arrived between tag and data";
   266	      (0, false)
   267	    end
   268	  else if tgt = t_sample then
   269	    match a land 3, Queue.peek_opt t.smp.fifo with
   270	    | 0, h -> t.smp_lat <- h <> None; ((match h with Some (_, n) -> 0x80 lor n | None -> 0), false)
   271	    | 1, Some (w, _) when t.smp_lat -> (w land 0xFF, false)
   272	    | 2, h ->
   273	      let lat = t.smp_lat in
   274	      t.smp_lat <- false;
   275	      (match h with Some (w, _) when lat -> ((w lsr 8) land 0xFF, true) | _ -> (0, false))
   276	    | _ -> (0, false)
   277	  else (0, false)
   278	
   279	(* the action of one written data byte *)
   280	let action_of ~tgt ~addr b =
   281	  let a = addr land 0xFFFF in
   282	  if tgt = t_reg then Some (A_reg (a, b))
   283	  else if tgt = t_prog then Some (A_prog (a, b))
   284	  else if tgt = t_bank then Some (A_bank (a, b))
   285	  else if tgt = t_hostin then Some (A_hostin b)
   286	  else if tgt = t_pecfg then Some (A_pecfg ((a lsr 8) land 3, b))
   287	  else if tgt = t_peinit then Some (A_peinit ((a lsr 8) land 3, b))
   288	  else if tgt = t_peseg then Some (A_peseg ((a lsr 2) land 3, a land 3, b))
   289	  else if tgt = t_stream then Some (A_stream (a land 3, b))
   290	  else if tgt = t_match then Some (A_match (b land 1))
   291	  else None
   292	
   293	(* ---- one clock ---- *)
   294	
   295	let reset_all t ~smp =
   296	  (* the core's clear loads the boot registers as they are during this clock *)
   297	  let boot_now = boot t in
   298	  Array.iteri (fun a _ -> t.regs.(a) <- reset_value a) t.regs;
   299	  t.err_mem <- false; t.hin_ovf <- false; t.hout_ovf <- false; t.act_drop <- false;
   300	  Queue.clear t.hostin; Queue.clear t.hostout; t.out_pend <- None; t.push_pend <- None;
   301	  Array.fill t.send_hi 0 4 false; Array.fill t.hold_st 0 4 0; Array.fill t.hold_w 0 4 0;
   302	  t.es <- ES.init (); t.es_out <- es_zero; t.es_last <- 0; t.crc <- 0; t.mtime <- 0; t.nco_acc <- 0; t.nco_out <- 0;
   303	  t.prev_s <- bit smp.(pad_hstb) 3; t.prev_r <- bit smp.(pad_hrd) 3;
   304	  t.nphase <- 0; t.hi_nib <- 0; t.pst <- Idle; t.op <- 0; t.tgt <- 0; t.addr <- 0; t.remaining <- 0;
   305	  t.rbuf <- 0; t.rphase <- 0; t.pend_action <- None; t.pend_fetch <- None;
   306	  t.prog_lo <- 0; t.str_lo <- 0; t.str_hi <- 0; t.hout_lat <- false; t.smp_lat <- false;
   307	  t.seq <- Isa2.Spec.reset ~boot:boot_now ~bank:t.bank;
   308	  t.str <- SM.create (streamer_cfg t);
   309	  t.smp <- PM.create (sampler_cfg t)
   310	
   311	let step t ~smp ~reset =
   312	  let out = outputs t in
   313	  let taps = taps t in
   314	  if reset then begin
   315	    (* the array has no reset: it runs on with idle inputs; segment 0's fixed port still shows the
   316	       edge sampler's output register during this clock *)
   317	    UM.cycle t.pe
   318	      { US.idle with fixed_d = [| es_level t; 0; 0; 0 |]; fixed_v = [| t.es_out.valid; false; false; false |] };
   319	    reset_all t ~smp;
   320	    out
   321	  end
   322	  else begin
   323	    let lvl i = bit smp.(i) 3 in
   324	    let running = run t and hold = held t and boot_now = boot t in
   325	    let flags = flags t taps in
   326	    let act = t.pend_action in
   327	    let reg_act a = match act with Some (A_reg (x, b)) when x = a -> Some b | _ -> None in
   328	    (* the host's fetch, from the state at the start of the clock *)
   329	    let fetched = Option.map (fetch t) t.pend_fetch in
   330	    (match t.pend_fetch, fetched with
   331	     | Some (tg, a), Some (b, _) -> t.last_fetch <- Some (tg, a, b)
   332	     | _ -> ());
   333	    (* sequencer *)
   334	    let pin_sel k = t.regs.(r_pinin k) land 15 in
   335	    let pin_in = Array.fold_left ( lor ) 0 (Array.init 8 (fun k -> lvl (pin_sel k) lsl k)) in
   336	    let pin_in4 = Array.fold_left ( lor ) 0 (Array.init 8 (fun k -> smp.(pin_sel k) lsl (4 * k))) in
   337	    let new_push = ref None and new_out = ref None and hostin_pop = ref false in
   338	    let port_pops = Array.make 4 false in
   339	    if running then begin
   340	      let host_ctl =
   341	        Option.map (fun b -> (b land 3, (b lsr 2) land 3, t.regs.(r_restart_pc))) (reg_act r_restart) in
   342	      let io =
   343	        Isa2.io ~pin_in4 ~host_in:(Option.value ~default:0 (Queue.peek_opt t.hostin))
   344	          ~host_in_valid:(not (Queue.is_empty t.hostin))
   345	          ~port_in:(Array.init 4 (fun p -> if t.hold_st.(p) = 2 then t.hold_w.(p) lsr 8 else t.hold_w.(p) land 0xFF))
   346	          ~port_in_valid:(Array.map (fun s -> s <> 0) t.hold_st) ~port_out_ready:(Array.make 4 true) ~flags
   347	          ?host_ctl pin_in
   348	      in
   349	      let w = t.prog.(Isa2.fetch_addr t.seq t.seq.thread land (t.cfg.prog_words - 1)) in
   350	      hit (Printf.sprintf "op %X" (w lsr 12));
   351	      let e = Isa2.step_f t.seq ~fetch:(fun a -> t.prog.(a land (t.cfg.prog_words - 1))) io in
   352	      if host_ctl <> None then hit "restart";
   353	      if e.host_in_ready then hit "IN from host";
   354	      (match e.port_pop with Some _ -> hit "RECV from a segment" | None -> ());
   355	      (match e.port_push with Some _ -> hit "SEND to a segment" | None -> ());
   356	      (match e.host_out with Some _ -> hit "OUT to host" | None -> ());
   357	      (match e.bank_write with Some _ -> hit "STB" | None -> ());
   358	      if flags <> 0 then hit "a flag input set";
   359	      if e.host_in_ready then hostin_pop := true;
   360	      (match e.port_pop with Some p -> port_pops.(p) <- true | None -> ());
   361	      new_push := e.port_push;
   362	      new_out := e.host_out
   363	    end;
   364	    (* the array: a thread's SEND (registered last clock) goes first; a host write waits *)
   365	    let mbx, host_mbx_done =
   366	      match t.push_pend, act with
   367	      | Some (p, b), _ -> ((true, p, (if t.send_hi.(p) then 1 else 0), b), false)
   368	      | None, Some (A_peseg (sg, sel, b)) -> hit "host feed write"; ((true, sg, sel, b), true)
   369	      | None, _ -> ((false, 0, 0, 0), false)
   370	    in
   371	    let mbx_wr, mbx_seg, mbx_sel, mbx_byte = mbx in
   372	    let cfg_w = match act with Some (A_pecfg (sg, b)) -> Some (sg, b) | _ -> None in
   373	    let init_w = match act with Some (A_peinit (sg, b)) -> Some (sg, b) | _ -> None in
   374	    UM.cycle t.pe
   375	      { mbx_wr; mbx_seg; mbx_sel; mbx_byte;
   376	        cfg_wr = cfg_w <> None; cfg_seg = Option.fold ~none:0 ~some:fst cfg_w; cfg_byte = Option.fold ~none:0 ~some:snd cfg_w;
   377	        init_wr = init_w <> None; init_seg = Option.fold ~none:0 ~some:fst init_w;
   378	        init_byte = Option.fold ~none:0 ~some:snd init_w;
   379	        fixed_d = [| es_level t; 0; 0; 0 |]; fixed_v = [| t.es_out.valid; false; false; false |] };
   380	    (* port glue: SEND byte order, RECV holding registers *)
   381	    let port_reset = Option.value ~default:0 (reg_act r_port_reset) in
   382	    (match t.push_pend with Some (p, _) -> t.send_hi.(p) <- not t.send_hi.(p) | None -> ());
   383	    for p = 0 to 3 do
   384	      if bit port_reset p = 1 then (t.send_hi.(p) <- false; t.hold_st.(p) <- 0)
   385	      else
   386	        match t.hold_st.(p) with
   387	        | 0 -> if taps.(p).tv then (hit "tap captured"; t.hold_st.(p) <- 1; t.hold_w.(p) <- taps.(p).td)
   388	        | 1 -> if port_pops.(p) then t.hold_st.(p) <- 2
   389	        | _ -> if port_pops.(p) then t.hold_st.(p) <- 0
   390	    done;
   391	    t.push_pend <- !new_push;
   392	    (* host FIFOs: pops before pushes, so a full FIFO takes a push in the clock its head leaves *)
   393	    if !hostin_pop then ignore (Queue.pop t.hostin);
   394	    (match act with
   395	     | Some (A_hostin b) -> hit "HOSTIN push"; if Queue.length t.hostin < hostin_depth then Queue.push b t.hostin else t.hin_ovf <- true
   396	     | _ -> ());
   397	    (match t.out_pend with
   398	     | Some e -> if Queue.length t.hostout < hostout_depth then Queue.push e t.hostout else t.hout_ovf <- true
   399	     | None -> ());
   400	    t.out_pend <- !new_out;
   401	    (* the assists *)
   402	    let smp_pop = match fetched with Some (_, p) -> p | None -> false in
   403	    let es_old = t.es_out and es_lvl = es_level t and c_residue_now = (crc_cfg t).residue in
   404	    let _, hit_now = matcher_now t in
   405	    if hold then begin
   406	      t.es <- ES.init (); t.es_out <- es_zero; t.es_last <- 0; t.crc <- 0; t.mtime <- 0; t.nco_acc <- 0; t.nco_out <- 0
   407	    end
   408	    else begin
   409	      if t.str.left > 0 || not (Queue.is_empty t.str.fifo) then hit "streamer busy";
   410	      SM.step t.str;
   411	      (match act with
   412	       | Some (A_stream (2, b)) -> SM.push ~count:(b land 15) t.str ((t.str_hi lsl 8) lor t.str_lo)
   413	       | _ -> ());
   414	      let pins =
   415	        if bit t.regs.(r_smp_src) 0 = 1 then es_lvl lor (Bool.to_int es_old.valid lsl 1)
   416	        else Array.fold_left ( lor ) 0 (Array.init 4 (fun j -> lvl (t.regs.(r_smp_pin j) land 15) lsl j))
   417	      in
   418	      let before = Queue.length t.smp.fifo in
   419	      ignore (PM.step t.smp ~pins ~pop:smp_pop);
   420	      if Queue.length t.smp.fifo > before then hit "sampler word";
   421	      let es_pad = t.regs.(r_es_pads) land 15 and act_pad = (t.regs.(r_es_pads) lsr 4) land 15 in
   422	      let active = bit t.regs.(r_es_mode) 3 = 0 || lvl act_pad = 1 in
   423	      if es_old.valid then hit "recovered bit";
   424	      if hit_now then hit "matcher hit";
   425	      if t.crc = c_residue_now then hit "CRC check true";
   426	      t.es_last <- es_lvl;
   427	      t.es_out <- ES.step_clock (es_cfg t) t.es ~active (List.init 4 (fun p -> bit smp.(es_pad) p));
   428	      let start = reg_act r_crc_start <> None || (bit t.regs.(r_crc_ctrl) 1 = 1 && hit_now) in
   429	      let c = crc_cfg t in
   430	      t.crc <- (if start then c.init else if es_old.valid then CRC.step c t.crc es_old.bit else t.crc);
   431	      if es_old.valid then begin
   432	        if t.mtime >= Array.length t.mhist then
   433	          t.mhist <- Array.append t.mhist (Array.make (Array.length t.mhist) 0);
   434	        t.mhist.(t.mtime) <- es_old.bit;
   435	        t.mtime <- t.mtime + 1
   436	      end;
   437	      (* the pin NCO (../multiphase/nco.ml): the levels of the accumulator at the clock's four
   438	         quarter points, registered; then the accumulator advances by inc *)
   439	      let inc = reg32 t r_nco_inc in
   440	      let a = [| t.nco_acc; u32 (t.nco_acc + (inc lsr 2)); u32 (t.nco_acc + (inc lsr 1));
   441	                 u32 (t.nco_acc + (inc lsr 1) + (inc lsr 2)) |] in
   442	      let l p = 1 - bit a.(p) 31 in
   443	      t.nco_out <- (l 0 lor (l 1 lsl 1) lor (l 2 lsl 2) lor (l 3 lsl 3))
   444	                   lor ((l 0 lor (l 0 lsl 1) lor (l 2 lsl 2) lor (l 2 lsl 3)) lsl 4)
   445	                   lor (rep4 (l 0) lsl 8);
   446	      t.nco_acc <- u32 (t.nco_acc + inc)
   447	    end;
   448	    (match act with
   449	     | Some (A_match b) -> for k = 36 downto 1 do t.chain.(k) <- t.chain.(k - 1) done; t.chain.(0) <- b
   450	     | _ -> ());
   451	    (* memories *)
   452	    (match act with
   453	     | Some (A_prog (a, b)) ->
   454	       if running then t.err_mem <- true
   455	       else if a land 1 = 0 then t.prog_lo <- b
   456	       else t.prog.((a lsr 1) land (t.cfg.prog_words - 1)) <- (b lsl 8) lor t.prog_lo
   457	     | Some (A_bank (a, b)) -> if running then t.err_mem <- true else t.bank.(a land (bank_bytes - 1)) <- b
   458	     | Some (A_stream (0, b)) -> t.str_lo <- b
   459	     | Some (A_stream (1, b)) -> t.str_hi <- b
   460	     | _ -> ());
   461	    (* the host link *)
   462	    (match fetched with Some (b, _) -> t.rbuf <- b | None -> ());
   463	    t.pend_fetch <- None;
   464	    let next_action = ref None in
   465	    let s = lvl pad_hstb and r = lvl pad_hrd in
   466	    let d = Array.fold_left ( lor ) 0 (Array.init 4 (fun k -> lvl (pad_hdata + k) lsl k)) in
   467	    if r <> t.prev_r then begin
   468	      (* raising R starts reading what a READ command fetched; lowering it ends the command *)
   469	      t.nphase <- 0; t.rphase <- 0;
   470	      if r = 0 then t.pst <- Idle
   471	    end
   472	    else if s <> t.prev_s then begin
   473	      if r = 0 then begin
   474	        if t.nphase = 0 then (t.hi_nib <- d; t.nphase <- 1)
   475	        else begin
   476	          t.nphase <- 0;
   477	          let b = (t.hi_nib lsl 4) lor d in
   478	          match t.pst with
   479	          | Idle ->
   480	            let op = b lsr 4 in
   481	            if op = op_write || op = op_read then (t.op <- op; t.tgt <- b land 15; t.pst <- Addr_hi)
   482	          | Addr_hi -> t.addr <- b lsl 8; t.pst <- Addr_lo
   483	          | Addr_lo -> t.addr <- t.addr lor b; t.pst <- Count
   484	          | Count ->
   485	            t.remaining <- b + 1;
   486	            if t.op = op_write then t.pst <- Wdata
   487	            else (t.pst <- Rdata; t.pend_fetch <- Some (t.tgt, t.addr))
   488	          | Wdata ->
   489	            next_action := action_of ~tgt:t.tgt ~addr:t.addr b;
   490	            t.addr <- (t.addr + 1) land 0xFFFF;
   491	            t.remaining <- t.remaining - 1;
   492	            if t.remaining = 0 then t.pst <- Idle
   493	          | Rdata -> t.pst <- Idle
   494	        end
   495	      end
   496	      else if t.rphase = 0 then t.rphase <- 1
   497	      else begin
   498	        t.rphase <- 0;
   499	        if t.pst = Rdata && t.remaining > 1 then begin
   500	          t.remaining <- t.remaining - 1;
   501	          t.addr <- (t.addr + 1) land 0xFFFF;
   502	          t.pend_fetch <- Some (t.tgt, t.addr)
   503	        end
   504	        else begin
   505	          t.remaining <- 0; t.rbuf <- 0;
   506	          if t.pst = Rdata then t.pst <- Idle
   507	        end
   508	      end
   509	    end;
   510	    t.prev_s <- s; t.prev_r <- r;
   511	    (* an action waits a clock when a thread's SEND took the array's write port; a newer byte
   512	       overwrites a waiting one, and that is recorded *)
   513	    let waiting = match act with Some (A_peseg _) when not host_mbx_done -> hit "host feed write waited for a SEND"; act | _ -> None in
   514	    (match !next_action, waiting with
   515	     | Some a, Some _ -> t.act_drop <- true; t.pend_action <- Some a
   516	     | Some a, None -> t.pend_action <- Some a
   517	     | None, w -> t.pend_action <- w);
   518	    (* register writes land at the end of the clock *)
   519	    (match act with
   520	     | Some (A_reg (a, b)) ->
   521	       if a = r_status then (t.err_mem <- false; t.hin_ovf <- false; t.hout_ovf <- false; t.act_drop <- false)
   522	       else if a < reg_space && rw_bits a > 0 then t.regs.(a) <- b land ((1 lsl rw_bits a) - 1)
   523	     | _ -> ());
   524	    (* a clock spent in clear leaves the sequencer in its reset state, from the boot registers as
   525	       they were during the clock; a held streamer and sampler are recreated with the
   526	       configuration as it is now, which is what their idle outputs show in the next clock *)
   527	    if not running then t.seq <- Isa2.Spec.reset ~boot:boot_now ~bank:t.bank;
   528	    if hold then begin
   529	      t.str <- SM.create (streamer_cfg t);
   530	      t.smp <- PM.create (sampler_cfg t)
   531	    end;
   532	    out
   533	  end

    46	let config = Chip_spec.default_config
    47	
    48	(* A synchronous single-port memory: [addr], [we] and [wdata] in one clock; the word at [addr]
    49	   is on the output during the next clock. *)
    50	type mem = clock:Signal.t -> size:int -> width:int -> addr:Signal.t -> we:Signal.t -> wdata:Signal.t -> Signal.t
    51	
    52	let behavioural_mem : mem =
    53	 fun ~clock ~size ~width:_ ~addr ~we ~wdata ->
    54	  let abits = address_bits_for size in
    55	  let a = select addr (abits - 1) 0 in
    56	  let q = memory size ~write_port:{ write_clock = clock; write_address = a; write_enable = we; write_data = wdata }
    57	      ~read_address:(reg (Reg_spec.create ~clock ()) a) in
    58	  q
    59	
    60	type mems = { prog_mem : mem; bank_mem : mem }
    61	
    62	let behavioural = { prog_mem = behavioural_mem; bank_mem = behavioural_mem }
    63	
    64	type outputs = { pad_nib : Signal.t; uio_oe : Signal.t; dbg : (string * Signal.t) list }
    65	
    66	(* a FIFO of [depth] entries; a push into a full FIFO is taken when the head leaves in the same
    67	   clock. Returns head, count, accept. *)
    68	let fifo spec ~depth ~push ~pop ~data =
    69	  let pbits = address_bits_for depth in
    70	  let rd = wire pbits and wr = wire pbits and count = wire (pbits + 1) in
    71	  let full = count ==:. depth and nonempty = count <>:. 0 in
    72	  let popping = pop &: nonempty in
    73	  let accept = push &: (~:full |: popping) in
    74	  let slots = List.init depth (fun i -> reg spec ~enable:(accept &: (wr ==:. i)) data) in
    75	  rd <== reg spec (mux2 popping (rd +:. 1) rd);
    76	  wr <== reg spec (mux2 accept (wr +:. 1) wr);
    77	  count <== reg spec (count +: uresize accept (pbits + 1) -: uresize popping (pbits + 1));
    78	  (mux rd slots, count, accept, full, nonempty)
    79	
    80	let onehot_index v = mux2 (bit v 0) (of_int ~width:2 0) (mux2 (bit v 1) (of_int ~width:2 1) (mux2 (bit v 2) (of_int ~width:2 2) (of_int ~width:2 3)))
    81	let rep4 b = repeat b 4
    82	let nib v i = select v (4 * i + 3) (4 * i)
    83	
    84	let create ?(cfg = config) ?(mems = behavioural) ~clock ~reset ~smp () =
    85	  let spec = Reg_spec.create ~clock ~clear:reset () in
    86	  let lvl i = bit smp (4 * i + 3) in
    87	  (* ---- the pending host action (decoded last clock), and the register file ---- *)
    88	  let act_valid = wire 1 and act_tgt = wire 4 and act_addr = wire 16 and act_byte = wire 8 in
    89	  let act_is tg = act_valid &: (act_tgt ==:. tg) &: ~:reset in
    90	  let act_reg_at a = act_is t_reg &: (act_addr ==:. a) in
    91	  let regs =
    92	    Array.init reg_space (fun a ->
    93	        let w = rw_bits a in

    95	        else
    96	          let rs = Reg_spec.override spec ~clear_to:(of_int ~width:8 (reset_value a)) in

   100	  let reg32 a = concat_msb [ regs.(a + 3); regs.(a + 2); regs.(a + 1); regs.(a) ] in

   138	  let open Always in
   139	  let nphase = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 and hi_nib = Variable.reg spec ~width:4 in
   140	  let pst = Variable.reg spec ~width:3 in

   142	  let is_read = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 and tgt = Variable.reg spec ~width:4 in
   143	  let addr = Variable.reg spec ~width:16 and remaining = Variable.reg spec ~width:9 in

   150	  let s_edge = s ^: prev_s and r_edge = rr ^: prev_r in
   151	  compile
   152	    [ if_ r_edge

   290	  in
   291	  (* the bit output is defined only while valid; hold the last one so that it is a level *)
   292	  let es_last = wire 1 in
   293	  let es_lvl = mux2 es_v es_b es_last in
   294	  es_last <== reg (Reg_spec.override spec ~clear:aclear) es_lvl;
   295	  es_bit <== es_lvl; es_valid <== es_v;
   296	  let match_y, match_hit =
   297	    Eth10.Matcher_en.create ~clock ~clear:aclear ~enable:es_v ~x:es_b ~cfg_in:(bit act_byte 0)
   298	      ~cfg_shift:(act_is t_match)
   299	  in
   300	  let crc_cfg : Eth10.Crc_unit.cfg_signals =
   301	    { s_poly = reg32 r_crc_poly; s_init = reg32 r_crc_init; s_mask = reg32 r_crc_mask;
   302	      s_refl = bit regs.(r_crc_ctrl) 0; s_xorout = reg32 r_crc_xorout; s_residue = reg32 r_crc_residue }
   303	  in
   304	  let crc_start = pulse r_crc_start |: (bit regs.(r_crc_ctrl) 1 &: match_hit) in
   305	  let crc_raw, _crc_value, crc_check =
   306	    Eth10.Crc_unit.create ~clock ~clear:aclear ~cfg:crc_cfg ~start:crc_start ~bit:es_b ~valid:es_v
   307	  in
   308	  let inc = reg32 r_nco_inc in
   309	  let inc = if is "nco_inc_bytes" then concat_msb [ select inc 31 16; select inc 7 0; select inc 15 8 ] else inc in
   310	  let nco = Mphase.Nco.create ~clock ~clear:aclear ~inc in
   311	  (* ---- flags ---- *)
   312	  let fsrc =
   313	    Array.to_list arr.tap_f @ Array.to_list arr.tap_v
   314	    @ [ es_v; es_lvl; es_in_burst; es_end; crc_check; match_hit; str_full; smp_valid; bit nco 0 ]
   315	  in
   316	  let fsrc = fsrc @ List.init (32 - List.length fsrc) (fun _ -> gnd) in
   317	  let flag f = mux (select regs.(r_flagsel (if is "flag_off_by_one" then (f + 1) mod 16 else f)) 4 0) fsrc in
   318	  let fl = concat_lsb (List.init 16 flag) in
   319	  (* thread t's flags are bits 4t..4t+3; the planted bug hands it thread t+1's *)
   320	  flags <== (if is "flag_thread_rot" then concat_msb [ select fl 3 0; select fl 15 4 ] else fl);
   321	  (* ---- fetch execution ---- *)
   322	  let sticky_err = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 and sticky_hin = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 in
   323	  let sticky_hout = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 and sticky_drop = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 in
   324	  let status =
   325	    concat_lsb [ sticky_err.value; sticky_hin.value; sticky_hout.value; hin_full; hout_ne; smp_valid; str_full;
   326	                 sticky_drop.value ] in
   327	  let read_reg a =
   328	    let cases =
   329	      List.filter_map
   330	        (fun x ->
   331	          let v =
   332	            if rw_bits x > 0 then Some regs.(x)
   333	            else if x = r_status then Some status
   334	            else if x = r_hostin_count then Some (uresize hin_count 8)
   335	            else if x = r_hostout_count then Some (uresize hout_count 8)
   336	            else if x >= r_crc_raw && x < r_crc_raw + 4 then Some (select crc_raw (8 * (x - r_crc_raw) + 7) (8 * (x - r_crc_raw)))
   337	            else if x = r_match then Some (concat_msb [ match_hit; zero 2; match_y ])
   338	            else if x = r_smp_overflows then Some smp_overflows
   339	            else None
   340	          in
   341	          Option.map (fun v -> (x, v)) v)
   342	        (List.init reg_space Fun.id)
   343	    in
   344	    let in_space = select a 15 7 ==:. 0 in
   345	    mux2 in_space (mux (select a 6 0) (List.init reg_space (fun x -> match List.assoc_opt x cases with Some v -> v | None -> zero 8))) (zero 8)
   346	  in
   347	  let prog_q = wire 16 and bank_q = wire 8 in
   348	  let fbyte =
   349	    mux ft
   350	      [ read_reg fa;
   351	        mux2 run (zero 8) (mux2 (bit fa 0) (select prog_q 15 8) (select prog_q 7 0));
   352	        mux2 run (zero 8) bank_q;
   353	        zero 8;
   354	        mux2 (bit fa 0)
   355	          (mux2 (if is "hostout_no_latch" then hout_ne else hout_lat.value) (select hout_head 7 0) (zero 8))
   356	          (mux2 hout_ne (concat_msb [ vdd; zero 4; select hout_head 10 8 ]) (zero 8));
   357	        zero 8; zero 8; zero 8; zero 8;
   358	        mux (select fa 1 0)
   359	          [ mux2 smp_valid (concat_msb [ vdd; zero 3; smp_count ]) (zero 8);
   360	            mux2 (smp_lat.value &: smp_valid) (select smp_data 7 0) (zero 8);
   361	            mux2 (smp_lat.value &: smp_valid) (select smp_data 15 8) (zero 8);
   362	            zero 8 ];
   363	        zero 8; zero 8; zero 8; zero 8; zero 8; zero 8 ]
   364	  in
   365	  hout_pop <== (fetch_is t_hostout &: bit fa 0 &: (if is "hostout_no_latch" then vdd else hout_lat.value));
   366	  smp_pop <== (fetch_is t_sample &: (select fa 1 0 ==:. 2) &: smp_lat.value &: ~:hold);
   367	  let mem_refused = run &: (fetch_is t_prog |: fetch_is t_bank |: act_is t_prog |: act_is t_bank) in
   368	  (* ---- memories ---- *)
   369	  let pwords = cfg.prog_words in
   370	  let host_prog_read = nf_valid.value &: (next_fetch_tgt ==:. t_prog) &: ~:run in
   371	  let host_prog_write = act_is t_prog &: ~:run &: bit act_addr 0 in
   372	  let prog_lo = Variable.reg spec ~width:8 in
   373	  let prog_word = if is "prog_byte_order" then concat_msb [ prog_lo.value; act_byte ] else concat_msb [ act_byte; prog_lo.value ] in
   374	  let prog_addr =
   375	    mux2 host_prog_write (srl act_addr 1) (mux2 host_prog_read (srl nf_addr.value 1) (uresize seq.imem_addr 16)) in
   376	  prog_q <== mems.prog_mem ~clock ~size:pwords ~width:16 ~addr:prog_addr ~we:host_prog_write ~wdata:prog_word;
   377	  imem_data <== prog_q;
   378	  let host_bank_read = nf_valid.value &: (next_fetch_tgt ==:. t_bank) &: ~:run in
   379	  let host_bank_write = act_is t_bank &: ~:run in
   380	  let bank_addr = mux2 core_owns_bank (uresize seq.bank_addr 16) (mux2 host_bank_write act_addr (mux2 host_bank_read nf_addr.value (zero 16))) in
   381	  bank_q <== mems.bank_mem ~clock ~size:Chip_spec.bank_bytes ~width:8 ~addr:bank_addr
   382	      ~we:(mux2 core_owns_bank core_bank_we host_bank_write) ~wdata:(mux2 core_owns_bank seq.bank_wdata act_byte);
   383	  bank_rdata <== bank_q;
   384	  (* ---- the pending action and fetch registers, and the glue registers ---- *)
   385	  let waiting = act_is t_peseg &: ~:host_mbx_done in
   386	  let a_valid = Variable.reg spec ~width:1 and a_tgt = Variable.reg spec ~width:4 in
   387	  let a_addr = Variable.reg spec ~width:16 and a_byte = Variable.reg spec ~width:8 in
   388	  act_valid <== a_valid.value; act_tgt <== a_tgt.value; act_addr <== a_addr.value; act_byte <== a_byte.value;
   389	  let clear_sticky = pulse r_status &: (if is "status_not_cleared" then gnd else vdd) in
   390	  compile
   391	    [ if_ na_valid.value
   392	        [ a_valid <--. 1; a_tgt <-- tgt.value; a_addr <-- addr.value; a_byte <-- na_byte.value
   393	        ; when_ waiting [ sticky_drop <--. 1 ] ]
   394	        [ a_valid <-- waiting ]
   395	    ; fetch_valid <-- nf_valid.value; fetch_tgt <-- next_fetch_tgt; fetch_addr <-- nf_addr.value
   396	    ; if_ rbuf_zero.value [ rbuf <--. 0 ] [ when_ fv [ rbuf <-- fbyte ] ]
   397	    ; when_ (fetch_is t_hostout)
   398	        [ if_ (bit fa 0) [ hout_lat <--. 0 ] [ hout_lat <-- hout_ne ] ]
   399	    ; when_ (fetch_is t_sample)
   400	        [ when_ (select fa 1 0 ==:. 0) [ smp_lat <-- smp_valid ]
   401	        ; when_ (select fa 1 0 ==:. 2) [ smp_lat <--. 0 ] ]
   402	    ; when_ (act_is t_prog &: ~:run &: ~:(bit act_addr 0)) [ prog_lo <-- act_byte ]
   403	    ; when_ (act_is t_stream &: (select act_addr 1 0 ==:. 0)) [ str_lo <-- act_byte ]
   404	    ; when_ (act_is t_stream &: (select act_addr 1 0 ==:. 1)) [ str_hi <-- act_byte ]
   405	    ; if_ clear_sticky
   406	        [ sticky_err <--. 0; sticky_hin <--. 0; sticky_hout <--. 0; sticky_drop <--. 0 ]
   407	        [ when_ mem_refused [ sticky_err <--. 1 ]
   408	        ; when_ (hin_push &: ~:hin_accept) [ sticky_hin <--. 1 ]
   409	        ; when_ (hout_push &: ~:hout_accept) [ sticky_hout <--. 1 ] ] ];
   410	  (* ---- pads ---- *)
   411	  let taps_f = arr.tap_f and taps_d = arr.tap_d in
   412	  let source sel =
   413	    let seq_pin k = (nib seq.pin_sub k, bit seq.pin_oe k) in
   414	    let seq_pin k = if is "pin_lane_swap" then seq_pin (match k with 0 -> 1 | 1 -> 0 | k -> k) else seq_pin k in
   415	    let seq_pin k =
   416	      let n, oe = seq_pin k in
   417	      if is "quarter_swap" then (concat_lsb [ bit n 0; bit n 2; bit n 1; bit n 3 ], oe) else (n, oe)
   418	    in
   419	    let srcs =
   420	      List.init 8 seq_pin
   421	      @ List.init 4 (fun k -> (rep4 (bit str_out k), bit str_oe k))
   422	      @ [ (select nco 3 0, vdd); (select nco 7 4, vdd); (select nco 11 8, vdd); (zero 4, vdd) ]
   423	      @ List.init 4 (fun j -> (rep4 taps_f.(j), vdd))
   424	      @ List.init 4 (fun j -> (rep4 (lsb taps_d.(j)), vdd))
   425	      @ [ (rep4 es_lvl, vdd) ]
   426	    in
   427	    let srcs = srcs @ List.init (32 - List.length srcs) (fun _ -> (zero 4, vdd)) in
   428	    (mux sel (List.map fst srcs), mux sel (List.map snd srcs))
   429	  in
   430	  let rnib =
   431	    if is "read_nibble_order" then mux2 (rphase.value ==:. 0) (select rbuf.value 3 0) (select rbuf.value 7 4)
   432	    else mux2 (rphase.value ==:. 0) (select rbuf.value 7 4) (select rbuf.value 3 0)
   433	  in
   434	  let pad_out = Array.make n_pads (zero 4) and oes = Array.make 8 gnd in
   435	  List.iter
   436	    (fun p ->
   437	      let sel = regs.(r_padsel p) in
   438	      let n, oe = source (select sel 4 0) in
   439	      let n = mux2 (bit sel 5) (~:n) n in
   440	      if p < 8 then pad_out.(p) <- mux2 oe n (rep4 (bit sel 6))
   441	      else (pad_out.(p) <- n; oes.(p - 8) <- oe))
   442	    general_out_pads;
   443	  for k = 0 to 3 do pad_out.(pad_hdata + k) <- rep4 (bit rnib k); oes.(k) <- prev_r done;
   444	  let oes = if is "uio_oe_swap" then (let o = Array.copy oes in o.(6) <- oes.(7); o.(7) <- oes.(6); o) else oes in
   445	  let dbg =
   446	    List.map (fun (n, s) -> ("seq_" ^ n, s)) seq.dbg
   447	    @ List.concat
   448	        (Array.to_list
   449	           (Array.mapi
   450	              (fun i (pe : Upe.Upe_rtl.pe_out) ->
   451	                [ Printf.sprintf "pe_s%d" i, pe.s; Printf.sprintf "pe_p%d" i, pe.p; Printf.sprintf "pe_pv%d" i, pe.pv;
   452	                  Printf.sprintf "pe_f%d" i, pe.f; Printf.sprintf "pe_l%d" i, pe.l; Printf.sprintf "pe_cfg%d" i, pe.cfg ])
   453	              arr.pes))
   454	    @ List.concat
   455	        (Array.to_list
   456	           (Array.mapi
   457	              (fun j (r : Upe.Upe_rtl.seg_regs) ->
   458	                [ Printf.sprintf "seg_flo%d" j, r.flo; Printf.sprintf "seg_fhi%d" j, r.fhi; Printf.sprintf "seg_fv%d" j, r.fv0;
   459	                  Printf.sprintf "seg_ctrl%d" j, r.ctrl; Printf.sprintf "seg_rep%d" j, r.rep;
   460	                  Printf.sprintf "seg_cnt%d" j, r.cnt; Printf.sprintf "seg_fw%d" j, r.fw ])
   461	              arr.segs))
   462	    @ [ "send_hi", concat_lsb (Array.to_list send_hi);
   463	        "holds", concat_lsb (Array.to_list (Array.map (fun (st, w) -> concat_msb [ w; st ]) holds));
   464	        "hin_count", hin_count; "hout_count", hout_count; "crc", crc_raw; "nco", nco; "match_y", match_y;
   465	        "rbuf", rbuf.value; "es", concat_msb [ es_b &: es_v; es_v; es_end; es_in_burst ]; "es_last", es_last ]
   466	  in
   467	  { pad_nib = concat_lsb (Array.to_list pad_out); uio_oe = concat_lsb (Array.to_list oes); dbg }
   468	
   469	let circuit ?cfg ?mems ?(debug = true) () =
   470	  let clock = input "clock" 1 and reset = input "reset" 1 and smp = input "smp" (4 * n_pads) in
   471	  let o = create ?cfg ?mems ~clock ~reset ~smp () in
   472	  Circuit.create_exn ~name:"chip_core"
   473	    ([ output "pad_nib" o.pad_nib; output "uio_oe" o.uio_oe ]
   474	     @ if debug then List.map (fun (n, s) -> output n s) o.dbg else [])

# Lean 4 Theorem Proving

Use this skill whenever you're editing Lean 4 proofs, debugging Lean builds, formalizing mathematics in Lean, or learning Lean 4 concepts. It prioritizes LSP-based inspection and mathlib search, with scripted primitives for sorry analysis, axiom checking, and error parsing.

## Core Principles

**Search before prove.** Many mathematical facts already exist in mathlib. Search exhaustively before writing tactics.

**Build incrementally.** Lean's type checker is your test suite—if it compiles with no sorries and standard axioms only, the proof is sound.

**Respect scope.** Follow the user's preference: fill one sorry, its transitive dependencies, all sorries in a file, or everything. Ask if unclear.

**Use 100-character line width for Lean files.** Do not wrap lines at 80 characters — Lean and mathlib convention is 100. If a line fits within 100 characters, keep it on one line. See [mathlib-style](references/mathlib-style.md) for breaking strategies when lines exceed 100.

**Mathlib style quick check.** For ordinary mathematical lambdas, write `fun x ↦ ...` (`\mapsto`), not `fun x => ...`. Use `=>` for `match`/`do` branches and metaprogramming callback idioms. Prefer `show P by tac` for tactic proofs; use `show P from term` only for term proofs. See [mathlib-style](references/mathlib-style.md).

**Preserve statements and signatures — they're the file's contract.** Theorem/lemma statements and type signatures are off-limits unless the user explicitly requests changes; changing them can break callers or alter the theorem being proved. If a proof seems to require changing a statement or adding a custom axiom, stop and discuss first because that changes the contract or the proof's trust basis. Exception: within synthesis wrappers (`/lean4:formalize`, `/lean4:autoformalize`), session-generated declarations may be redrafted under the outer-loop statement-safety rules; see cycle-engine.md.

**Docstrings are scoped by workflow.** Existing docstrings are API. The default is **Rule A** — any workflow that mutates existing declarations follows it unless the user explicitly requests docstring changes:

| Mode | May do | Boundary |
|------|--------|----------|
| **A — editing existing declarations** (e.g. `/lean4:prove`, `sorry-filler-deep`, golf, refactor, any agent editing existing decls) | adjust inline comments in proof bodies | never rewrite an existing docstring — explicit user request only |
| **B — review** (`/lean4:review`) | flag weak/missing docstrings and propose replacement text in the report | read-only — never mutates files |
| **C — new-file / new-decl generation** (`/lean4:draft`, `/lean4:formalize`, `/lean4:autoformalize`) | emit module/declaration docstrings on files or declarations it newly creates | existing docstrings stay under Rule A |

Per-command policy lives in each command's doc. The docstring split applies in every project; when the mathlib Template Gate selects the mathlib header, Rule C fills its module-docstring slot — Rule C neither selects nor redefines the [template](references/mathlib-style.md#1-file-header-copyright-module-imports-critical).

## Commands

| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `/lean4:draft` | Draft Lean declaration skeletons from informal claims |
| `/lean4:formalize` | Interactive formalization — drafting plus guided proving |
| `/lean4:autoformalize` | Autonomous end-to-end formalization from informal sources |
| `/lean4:prove` | Guided cycle-by-cycle theorem proving with explicit checkpoints |
| `/lean4:autoprove` | Autonomous multi-cycle theorem proving with explicit stop budgets |
| `/lean4:disprove` | Guided counterexample search with certified refutation |
| `/lean4:checkpoint` | Save progress with a safe commit checkpoint |
| `/lean4:review` | Read-only code review of Lean proofs |
| `/lean4:refactor` | Leverage mathlib, extract helpers, simplify proof strategies |
| `/lean4:golf` | Improve Lean proofs for directness, clarity, performance, and brevity |
| `/lean4:learn` | Interactive teaching and mathlib exploration |
| `/lean4:diagnose` | Diagnostics, cleanup, and migration help |

`/lean4:*` names are the native plugin's command aliases and also serve
as stable workflow names throughout this documentation. On hosts
without command registration (skill-only or portable installs), invoke
the `lean4` skill with your host's normal syntax and ask for the named
workflow — e.g., "Use the guided `prove` workflow on `Foo.lean:42`."

This plugin ships a host-agnostic parser (`lib/command_args/`) that covers the
parser-decidable startup rules of the seven parameter-heavy commands (`draft`,
`learn`, `formalize`, `autoformalize`, `prove`, `autoprove`, `disprove`). A small set of
documented startup rules in these commands depend on runtime context (repo-
level search, interactive prompting) and are applied by the command after
reading the parser's output. The other commands (`checkpoint`, `review`,
`refactor`, `golf`, `diagnose`) remain model-parsed.
When a host adapter installs the `UserPromptSubmit` hook, the parser runs
before the model sees a `/lean4:*` prompt matching one of the seven covered
commands, injects a `validated-invocation` block into context, and rejects
invalid invocations at the hook level; invocations of the other commands pass
through unchanged. Hosts without the hook fall back to model-parsed startup
via the shared [command-invocation.md](references/command-invocation.md)
contract.
Commands always announce resolved inputs, reject invalid startup configs before
doing work, and treat wall-clock budgets like `--max-total-runtime` as
best-effort.

### Which Command?

| Situation | Command |
|-----------|---------|
| Draft a Lean skeleton (skeleton by default) | `/lean4:draft` |
| Draft + prove interactively | `/lean4:formalize` |
| Filling sorries (interactive) | `/lean4:prove` |
| Filling sorries (unattended) | `/lean4:autoprove` |
| Searching for a counterexample to refute a claim | `/lean4:disprove` |
| Save point (per-file + project build, best-effort axiom scan, commit) | `/lean4:checkpoint` |
| Quality check (read-only) | `/lean4:review` |
| Simplify proof strategies (mathlib leverage, helpers) | `/lean4:refactor` |
| Optimizing compiled proofs | `/lean4:golf` |
| New to this project / exploring | `/lean4:learn --mode=repo` |
| Navigating mathlib for a topic | `/lean4:learn --mode=mathlib` |
| Something not working | `/lean4:diagnose` |
| Formalize + prove end-to-end (unattended) | `/lean4:autoformalize --source=... --claim-select=first --out=...` |

## Contributing (lean4-contribute plugin)

**If the `lean4-contribute` plugin is installed,** you may **suggest** these commands at natural stopping points. Rules:

- **Suggest first, never invoke unprompted.** Offer a one-line question; do not start the command flow.
- **Only invoke after explicit user opt-in** in the current conversation. Silence, topic change, or implicit frustration do not count as consent.
- **At most once per topic per session** unless the user engages.
- **Never mid-proof.** Wait for a natural stopping point.

| Situation | Suggest |
|-----------|---------|
| Problem appears to be in lean4-skills itself (wrong command behavior, contradictory docs, broken lint, bad guardrail, confusing plugin UX) — not ordinary Lean/mathlib/user-proof problems | "This looks like a lean4-skills bug. Want me to draft a bug report?" → `/lean4-contribute:bug-report` |
| User wants a workflow the plugin doesn't support, says a command should behave differently, or you must recommend awkward manual steps due to a missing feature | "This looks like a plugin workflow gap. Want me to draft a feature request?" → `/lean4-contribute:feature-request` |
| Result seems reusable beyond the current task: tactic-selection heuristic, mathlib search pattern, anti-pattern, documentation gap with a clear lesson — not one-off theorem facts or private repo details | "That seems reusable beyond this task. Want me to draft a shareable insight?" → `/lean4-contribute:share-insight` |

**If the plugin is not installed** and the user clearly hit a lean4-skills bug, workflow gap, or reusable insight (same criteria as above — not ordinary Lean/mathlib issues), you may offer the install hint once:

- At most once per session. Do not repeat if the user declined, ignored it, or moved on.
- Never mid-proof or during an active debugging loop.
- One short line, not a pitch: "If you want, install the `lean4-contribute` plugin and I can draft that report for you here." See the [lean4-contribute README](https://github.com/cameronfreer/lean4-skills/blob/main/plugins/lean4-contribute/README.md#installation) for setup.

## Typical Workflow

```
┌─ Entry points (pick one) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ /lean4:draft              Skeleton by default (--mode=attempt for shallow proof)   │
│ /lean4:formalize          Interactive: draft + guided proving                      │
│ /lean4:autoformalize      Autonomous: draft + autonomous proving                   │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        ↓ (if sorries remain)
/lean4:prove / autoprove    Proof engines (sorry filling, no header edits)
        ↓
/lean4:refactor            Leverage mathlib, extract helpers (optional)
        ↓
/lean4:golf                Improve proofs (optional)
        ↓
/lean4:checkpoint          Save point (per-file + project build)
```

Use `/lean4:learn` at any point to explore repo structure or navigate mathlib. Three entry points: `/lean4:draft` for skeletons, `/lean4:formalize` for interactive synthesis (draft + guided proving), `/lean4:autoformalize` for unattended source-to-proof.

**Refutation branch:** Use `/lean4:disprove <target>` when the goal is to **refute** a statement rather than prove it. Always interactive; runs a 6-phase cycle (Plan → Work → Checkpoint → Review → Accumulate → Continue/Stop) where Phase 1 generates dynamic Step 0 / Step 1 / Step 2 menus seeded by accumulated evidence (Phase 5 — Accumulate — replaces prove's Replan). Each cycle is a widening search pass over the same target. Append-only — it adds a `T_counterexample` theorem alongside the original sorry, never rewrites the original declaration. Requires Python 3.11+ (registry loader). See [disprove-engine.md](references/disprove-engine.md), incl. its [Implementation Status](references/disprove-engine.md#implementation-status) table (deterministic vs model-mediated vs deferred).

**Notes:**
- `/lean4:prove` asks before each cycle; `/lean4:autoprove` loops autonomously with explicit stop budgets
- Both trigger `/lean4:review` at configured intervals (`--review-every`)
- When reviews run (via `--review-every`), they act as gates: review → replan → continue. In prove, replan requires user approval; in autoprove, replan auto-continues
- Review supports `--mode=batch` (default) or `--mode=stuck` (triage); review is always read-only
- `/lean4:autoformalize` wraps draft+autoprove in a single command (source → claims → skeletons → proofs); replaces `autoprove --formalize=auto`
- Proof engines (`prove`/`autoprove`) never modify declaration headers (header fence)
- `/lean4:disprove` reports `REFUTED` only when Lean typechecks the negation; otherwise `WITNESS_UNCERTIFIED` or `INCONCLUSIVE`
- If you hit environment issues, run `/lean4:diagnose` to diagnose

## LSP Tools (Preferred)

Sub-second feedback and search tools (LeanSearch, Loogle, LeanFinder) via Lean LSP MCP:

```
lean_goal(file, line)                           # See exact goal
lean_hover_info(file, line, col)                # Understand types
lean_local_search("keyword")                    # Fast local + mathlib (unlimited)
lean_leanfinder("goal or query")                # Semantic, goal-aware (10/30s)
lean_leansearch("natural language")             # Semantic search (3/30s)
lean_loogle("?a → ?b → _")                      # Type-pattern (unlimited if local mode)
lean_hammer_premise(file, line, col)            # Premise suggestions for simp/aesop/grind (3/30s)
lean_state_search(file, line, col)              # Goal-conditioned lemma search (3/30s)
lean_multi_attempt(file, line, snippets=[...])  # Test multiple tactics
lean_diagnostic_messages(file)                  # Per-file error/warning check
lean_code_actions(file, line)                   # Resolve "Try this" suggestions to edits
```

`lean_run_code` is for isolated scratch experiments, not a substitute for live proof-state inspection via `lean_goal`/`lean_multi_attempt`/`lean_diagnostic_messages`. Prefer live-file tools when the question depends on actual file context.

## Capabilities

| Capability | Required | Check | Fallback |
|-----------|----------|-------|----------|
| Lean / Lake | yes | `lean --version`, `lake --version` | none — run `/lean4:diagnose` |
| Python 3 | yes (scripts) | `python3 --version` or persistent `$LEAN4_PYTHON_BIN` | none for script-dependent operations |
| Helper runtime path | yes (scripts) | trusted Codex `bin_dir`/`scripts_dir`, or persistent `$LEAN4_SCRIPTS` | use the diagnose workflow; stay LSP-only if unresolved |
| Lean LSP MCP | no | try `lean_goal` on any `.lean` file | scripts + `lake env lean` (file-level only) |
| `lean_run_code` | no | try calling it | `lake env lean` on temp file |
| `lean_code_actions` | no | try calling it | manual "Try this" application |
| Subagent dispatch | no | host-dependent | run work in main thread |
| Slash commands | no | host-dependent | follow skill instructions directly |

## Operating Profiles

The skill adapts to what's available. Determine your profile by checking capabilities above, then follow the corresponding guidance.

### Runtime path resolution

Resolve the helper runtime in this order:

1. **Trusted native Codex plugin:** SessionStart context contains
   `lean4_plugin_runtime=codex`, absolute `bin_dir` / `scripts_dir` paths,
   and `shell_env_persistent=false`. Substitute those literal absolute paths
   into helper commands. They are context values, not shell variables: invoke
   `/absolute/bin/lean4-skills-…`, never `$bin_dir/…`, and do not expect bare
   wrappers on PATH.
2. **Persistent environment:** use `$LEAN4_PLUGIN_ROOT`, `$LEAN4_SCRIPTS`,
   `$LEAN4_REFS`, and bare wrappers verified on PATH.
3. **No helper runtime:** remain in the LSP-backed core skill and skip
   script-dependent steps until the installation is repaired or upgraded.

Do not emit `$LEAN4_BIN/...` or `$LEAN4_SCRIPTS/...` commands when those shell
variables are unset. A trusted Codex absolute path is a complete alternative,
not evidence that persistent variables exist.

### full (all capabilities)

MCP + subagents + commands. Full workflow with live goal inspection, tactic testing, and parallel subagent dispatch (requires disjoint owned-file sets per agent, or separate worktrees). Subagents get pre-collected MCP context per [cycle-engine.md § Pre-flight Context](references/cycle-engine.md#pre-flight-context-for-subagent-dispatch). If `lean_run_code` is unavailable, use `/tmp` scratch files with `lake env lean` for isolated experiments.

### mcp_main_only (MCP available, no subagent dispatch)

MCP works in the main thread. Run all proof work directly — do not delegate to subagents. All cycle-engine phases execute in-thread. If `lean_run_code` is unavailable, use `/tmp` scratch files with `lake env lean` for isolated experiments.

### scripts_only (no MCP, no subagents)

Use the resolved helper runtime (`scripts_dir` under native Codex,
`$LEAN4_SCRIPTS` under a persistent environment) for search and use
`lake env lean` / `lake build` for validation. **Key limitations in this mode:**
- **No live goal inspection** — `lean_goal` is unavailable; you can read the file and check compilation output, but cannot see proof state at a specific line
- **No tactic testing** — `lean_multi_attempt` is unavailable; edits must be validated by compiling the file (`lake env lean`)
- **No real-time diagnostics** — `lean_diagnostic_messages` is unavailable; use `lake env lean <file>` (from project root) for compilation errors, but feedback is file-level, not line-level
- **Search is script-based** — `lean4-skills-smart-search` replaces LSP search tools

This mode is functional for straightforward proofs but significantly slower and less precise than MCP-backed workflows.

### review_only (read-only, no edits)

Read proof state and assess quality. No edits, no commits, no subagent dispatch.

## File Handling Rules

**Scratch-work ladder** (in preference order):
1. Live file + MCP tools (`lean_goal`, `lean_multi_attempt`, `lean_diagnostic_messages`)
2. `lean_run_code` for isolated experiments
3. `/tmp` scratch files only when `lean_run_code` is unavailable and the experiment must not touch the live file
4. Never create scratch files in the repo root

**File inspection:** Use direct file-read and search tools for source files — for example Read/Grep when available, or line-range reads and `rg` in shell. Do not spin up Python scripts, temp files, or `cat` pipelines just to read accessible lines; reserve scripts for real parsing, transformation, or multi-file analysis that direct read/search tools cannot handle cleanly.

**Staging:** Stage only files touched during the current session. Never use `git add -A` or broad glob patterns. Print the exact staged set before committing.

See [sorry-filling.md](references/sorry-filling.md) for the full scratch-work preference order.

     1	(* Executable specification of the whole chip core, one clock per [step].
     2	
     3	   It is composed from the blocks' own models, which are not written anew here:
     4	   - the sequencer: ISA v2's interpreter (Isa2, ../sequencer-v2/isa2.ml);
     5	   - the PE array: Upe.Model (../unified-pe/verify/model.ml);
     6	   - the streamer and the sampler: Pstream.Model and Psample.Model;
     7	   - the edge-tracking sampler and the CRC unit: the models in Eth10.Edge_sampler and
     8	     Eth10.Crc_unit; the matcher: the closed form of Eth10.Model.
     9	   New here, because nothing specified them before: the glue (host link, its FIFOs, the register
    10	   file, the pin map and pad sources, the flag selects, the port holds between the threads and
    11	   the array, the memories) and the pin NCO, which has RTL (../multiphase/nco.ml) but no model.
    12	   The NCO's behaviour is written below from nco.ml's header comment.
    13	
    14	   The boundary is the four-phase stage's (README, "Phases"): inputs are, per input pad, the
    15	   four quarter samples the core sees in this clock (bit p = quarter p); outputs are, per output
    16	   pad, the nibble of levels for the next clock's four quarters, and the uio output enables.
    17	   Outputs depend only on the state at the start of the clock.
    18	
    19	   Within a clock everything reads the state as it was at the start of the clock, and every
    20	   update lands at its end, as registers do. Where models must be stepped in some order, the
    21	   order below is the one in which their inputs become available, and no model reads another's
    22	   updated state. *)
    23	
    24	open Regs
    25	module UM = Upe.Model
    26	module US = Upe.Spec
    27	module SM = Pstream.Model

    37	let hostin_depth = 16 and hostout_depth = 8
    38	
    39	(* a host-link byte that was decoded in the previous clock and acts in this one *)
    40	type action =
    41	  | A_reg of int * int            (* address, byte *)
    42	  | A_prog of int * int
    43	  | A_bank of int * int
    44	  | A_hostin of int
    45	  | A_pecfg of int * int          (* segment, byte *)

    48	  | A_stream of int * int         (* address mod 4, byte *)
    49	  | A_match of int                (* bit *)
    50	
    51	type parser = Idle | Addr_hi | Addr_lo | Count | Wdata | Rdata
    52	
    53	type t = {
    54	  cfg : config;
    55	  regs : int array;
    56	  mutable err_mem : bool; mutable hin_ovf : bool; mutable hout_ovf : bool; mutable act_drop : bool;

    58	  bank : int array;

    63	  mutable push_pend : (int * int) option;    (* the core's SEND to a port, registered *)
    64	  send_hi : bool array;                      (* per port: the next SEND byte is the high byte *)

    82	  mutable pst : parser; mutable op : int; mutable tgt : int; mutable addr : int;
    83	  mutable remaining : int;
